# 495: Chapter 492: Guiding Kubfu, Capturing Chi-Yu

"Beah..."

Upon hearing the person's words, Kubfu felt a bit embarrassed.

Because the training method it was currently using was devised by this human.

Initially, it thought the other had left, which is why it sneaked out to try it, but was unexpectedly caught in the act...

Feeling shy and flustered, Kubfu briskly jumped out from under the waterfall, seemingly trying to escape.

As this adorable Legendary Pokemon was about to run away, D quickly shouted:

"Wait!"

Kubfu paused its movements and turned around, looking over curiously: "Beah?"

"You can keep practicing here, we won't disturb you at all." D conveyed his goodwill to Kubfu.

This was his first time seeing a wild Kubfu, so he definitely wanted to observe it properly.

Sure enough, after hearing his words, Kubfu seemed to abandon its intention of fleeing.

It glanced at D and then at the waterfall, pondering something in its mind.

After a while, as if reaching a certain decision, Kubfu turned back and resumed its practice under the waterfall.

"Boni!" On the side, Ogerpon watched the small Pokemon persevering under the waterfall and felt shocked yet impressed.

It's so hardworking!

"That's right." D understood Ogerpon's sentiment and nodded, saying: "Pokemons like Kubfu diligently train their body and mind, engaging in rigorous practice day by day, meticulously honing their Moves."

They are exceptionally serious Pokemons, unafraid of hardship, capable of enduring hardship, and practice earnestly to enhance their strength.

Interestingly, this kind of Pokemon has two distinct evolutionary forms.

One is adept at one-hit kills, dashing into the opponent's space with a finely honed punch as a Single Strike Urshifu.

The other constantly unleashes flowing, continuous Moves, making multiple hits their creed as a Rapid Strike Urshifu.

Observing Kubfu's diligent practice, Cyclizar appeared to be inspired by it.

It displayed a serious expression: "Ga-ow!"

Then it joined under the waterfall to resume its practice.

"Cyclizar is working hard too!" D couldn't help but voice admiration.

Nearby, Pawmot and Dragonite excitedly cheered Cyclizar on.

"Bamo!"

"Awoo!"

"Boni～"

Koraidon just looked speechless, then lazily lay on the grass, yawning.

"Aren't you all going to train?" D glanced at the Pokemons beside him, asking.

"Awoo!" Dragonite shook its big head, looking rather confused.

Because it felt like practicing under the waterfall was equivalent to simply getting drenched, with no other sensation.

Dragonite didn't understand, nor could Flutter Mane from the antiquity grasp the purpose of waterfall training.

D: "..."

He suddenly felt Cyclizar might just be standing there mindlessly letting the waterfall rinse its head.

After contemplating for a moment, D was inspired by the Rapid Strike Urshifu's training method and stepped forward to guide Cyclizar.

"Pay attention, Cyclizar, waterfall training isn't just about letting the waterfall wash over your body; you have to learn to perceive during this process."

Beneath the waterfall, Cyclizar and the nearby Kubfu simultaneously opened their eyes to look over.

"Use your heart to perceive the power of the water flow; sometimes it becomes rapid and rushing, while other times it flows smoothly like a quietly trickling mirror."

"Ga-ow..." Cyclizar listened carefully.

"Imagine it as a battle; if you can ignore the waterfall's impact, you'll gain the ability to face more intense battles."

After speaking, D suddenly noticed the Kubfu on the other side also attentively listening.

He paused and turned to Kubfu, saying: "This is effective for you too, Kubfu."

After all, this is originally Kubfu's special training method; only by fully understanding and mastering the essence of water flow can it utilize this power to evolve into a Rapid Strike Urshifu.

Startled by the human speaking to it, Kubfu quickly closed its eyes, pretending to resume practicing.

Yet soon, it immersed itself in the words the human had spoken, feeling the power of the surrounding water flow with its heart...

Kubfu seemed to enter a state of enlightenment.

Witnessing this, D didn't disturb it, instead taking out his phone to film a precious ecological video of the wild Kubfu practicing.

At this moment, the Pokemon tasked by D returned from searching for the stakes and shrine.

D instructed Dragonite and others to guard Cyclizar's practice while he went with Koraidon to extract the stake.

Time ticked away steadily, and D spent the entire morning pulling out the seven stakes sealing Chi-Yu.

With Gastly's help, he also discovered the Firescourge Shrine hidden behind a waterfall.

After this, there was only one last stake left to lift Chi-Yu's seal.

At noon, to thank these wild Pokemons for their assistance, D specially prepared a sumptuous Pokeblock feast for them.

A group of Pokemons gathered and happily enjoyed the delicious meal.

---

D went to the side and helped Cyclizar dry off with a dry towel.

Cyclizar obediently stretched its arms and raised its head, allowing D to dry its body, revealing a comfortable expression on its face.

Not far away, Kubfu, who had also come ashore, shook the water from its fur vigorously.

The water flicked off splashed towards D.

Kubfu: Σ(ﾟ∀ﾟﾉ)ﾉ

"Hmm?" D smiled and reassured, "Don't worry, it's okay. Why don't you join us for lunch?"

"Beya..."

Kubfu looked up dazedly.

Just when D thought Kubfu would happily join, the little bear suddenly became shy.

It fiddled with its small, short paws, seemingly unsure of where to put them.

Finally, Kubfu suddenly looked towards the nearby Bamboo Forest.

"Beya!"

It quickly ran over, pulled out a bamboo shoot growing nearby, and offered it to D.

[Kubfu's Grateful Bamboo Shoot: A special ingredient, a gift offered by Kubfu with a grateful heart, which may have unexpected effects when used in cooking.]

Looking at the bamboo shoot in his hand, D was slightly taken aback, "For me?"

"Beya!" Kubfu seemed a bit embarrassed.

D immediately understood, realizing that Kubfu was expressing gratitude for his guidance earlier!

"I get it, I really like your gift." D said softly.

Kubfu touched its head in embarrassment, "Beya～"

"Then join us for a meal!"

D brought over a plate of snacks for Kubfu.

Sitting cross-legged on the grass, Kubfu curiously picked up a piece of snack with its hand before tasting it.

In the next moment, Kubfu's eyes lit up brightly.

"Beya!"

"Gao～"

Cyclizar had fond feelings toward this little Bear-type Pokemon practicing with it under the waterfall.

It eagerly introduced, "Gao!" (These are all handmade by D!)

"Beya?" D? Is that the human's name?

Cyclizar nodded affirmatively, "Gao!" (That's right!)

D sat beside Koraidon, eating a sandwich while observing Cyclizar and Kubfu's interaction.

"They really seem to get along well!"

Koraidon: "Agassi..."

But come to think of it, Kubfu really is adorable!

Such a serious-tempered little Bear-type Pokemon, and it's shy too.

D pondered the idea of inviting Kubfu to join them.

He quietly whispered to Koraidon, "Koraidon, do you think Kubfu would agree to come back to the ranch and live with us?"

Koraidon confidently replied, "Agassi!"

Surprised by Koraidon's certainty, D asked, "How do you know?"

Koraidon replied matter-of-factly, "Agassi!" Because D is the world's best Trainer!

D: "..."

That may not be exactly true, because to every Pokemon, their own Trainer is unique.

A Pokemon like Kubfu is enthusiastic about physical training.

Although the ranch doesn't have a suitable waterfall for training right now, D thought he could find an alternative.

Perhaps it could learn about the mysteries of water flow at Sea God Kyogre's habitat, and keep Cyclizar company too.

The two Pokemon could do special training together.

But for now, there are more pressing matters to attend to.

After lunch, D began the search for the last stake.

He intended to continue enlisting the Pokemon's help for the search.

But upon hearing this, Kubfu suddenly spoke up:

"Beya?"

Through the Power of Viridian, D sensed Kubfu's thoughts.

It was asking: Are you looking for a glowing stake?

D was slightly surprised, then asked, "Do you know where it is?"

"Beya!"

Kubfu nodded its head, then led the way ahead.

D quickly caught up. Under Kubfu's guidance, he soon discovered the last stake that sealed Chi-Yu in a hidden cave.

The entrance of the cave was covered with yellowed bamboo leaves, making it look very inconspicuous, no wonder Gastly and the others didn't find this place before.

According to Kubfu, this small cave was discovered during a rain shelter.

The entrance of the cave was only about thirty centimeters in diameter, extremely narrow. D couldn't enter, so he asked Kubfu to go in and help pull out the stake.

"Bayah!"

Kubfu nodded its small head and agreed.

Standing only sixty centimeters tall, Kubfu easily slipped into the cave.

Before long, as the last stake was removed, a silent sound echoed from afar.

The surrounding Pokemon heard the sound too, turning their heads to look around, confused at each other.

The seal on Chi-Yu has been lifted!

To avoid Chi-Yu breaking the seal and causing calamity, D briefly explained the situation to the group of Pokemon, asking them to wait here while he rode Koraidon swiftly toward that direction.

Watching the scene, Kubfu sensed something bad was about to happen and decisively followed along.

The Firescourge Shrine was located behind an inconspicuous waterfall.

The waterfall cascaded down from the cliff above, forming a spacious lake below.

When D arrived, fog was rising over the lake.

Reflections of red firelight were cast behind the waterfall; the fog was steam produced from the lake water evaporating at high temperatures!

Suddenly, a red shadow appeared on the lake surface.

The next moment, D saw a Pokemon swimming leisurely in the water.

It was a Pokemon shaped like a goldfish, mostly yellow, with its tail and fins like clusters of burning flames.

Its pupils were large and black, taking up most of its eyes, each surrounded by two beads that faded from the hole-end.

At this moment, Chi-Yu also noticed the intruding human and Pokemon.

The beads around its eyes suddenly flashed with a short burst of golden light accompanied by a red glow.

This was proof that Chi-Yu was in an excited state!

The next second, rolling heat waves swept in, golden-red flames enveloped the entire lake, making the whole lake seem like a sea of lava.

Steam generated by the melting lake water spread all around as Chi-Yu's figure vanished within.

"Koraidon, prepare for battle!" D said in a deep voice.

"Gaaah!"

Koraidon replied reliably.

Then it transformed into its standing form, scarlet energy flowed over its crimson body.

Koraidon stomped the ground with its right foot, letting out an ancient roar.

In an instant, the ground cracked with black fissures, extending toward the lake where Chi-Yu was.

Bulldoze!

A powerful shockwave instantly stirred massive waves, and the Chi-Yu in the water was also shaken out.

Under dazzling sunlight, Koraidon locked onto Chi-Yu in mid-air through the diffusing mist with its golden eyes.

The next moment, its body shot out swiftly, transforming while launching a fierce Tackle at Chi-Yu.

Terror-inducing ancient power instantly triggered a frightful explosion.

Chi-Yu, unable to react, fell into the water.

A moment later, the unconscious Chi-Yu floated to the water's surface.

Koraidon looked back at D.

D, in sync, took out a Poke Ball and threw it at Chi-Yu.

The Poke Ball struck Chi-Yu, accompanied by a click, automatically opening to capture Chi-Yu inside, then closed automatically, gently floating on the lake surface.

The Poke Ball's button flashed with red light.

Once, twice, three times, four times...

Until the fifth time, the flashing red light disappeared, and the Poke Ball stopped moving.

This signified a successful capture!

The Poke Ball containing Chi-Yu gradually floated to the shore along with the lake's ripples.

D stepped forward to pick it up, exhaling deeply.

With this, only the last Wo-Chien and Chien-Pao remain among the four Ruinous Pokemon!

In the Bamboo Forest, the following Kubfu looked down at D and the Pokemon named Koraidon, shocked.

In just one encounter, that Pokemon who melted lake water and evaporated the waterfall was defeated...

Amazing, truly amazing!

....
